<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We construct a finitely generated residually finite group of integral cohomological dimension two and geometric dimension three, disproving the Eilenberg–Ganea conjecture.

- **stmt-thm-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There is a finitely generated residually finite group $G$ such that $$\operatorname{cd}_{\mathbb Z}G=2,\qquad \operatorname{gd}G=3.$$ One may take $G$ to be the height kernel in the right-angled Artin group associated to a finite acyclic flag triangulation of the presentation complex $$\label{eq:seed-presentation-intro} \langle x,y\mid x^2=y^5,\ x^2=(xy^{-1})^3\rangle.$$ No two-dimensional classifying space for this $G$ exists, regardless of the number of its cells.
  - Explanation: The construction separates integral cohomological dimension from geometric dimension and excludes every two-dimensional classifying space.
- **stmt-lem-localized-degree** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-localized-degree)): Let $m\ge1$, and let $f:H^m\to H^m$ be a word map with $\det B\ne0$. Suppose that $\mathcal O\subset H^m$ is open, invariant under simultaneous conjugation, contains $\mathbf 1$, and satisfies $$\partial\mathcal O\cap f^{-1}(\mathbf 1)=\varnothing.$$ Then $\deg(f,\mathcal O,\mathbf 1)\ne0$.
  - Explanation: A nonsingular exponent-sum matrix forces nonzero degree in the stated invariant isolating region.
- **stmt-auto-remark-1** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-auto-remark-1)): The same argument works for arbitrarily large odd primes, so it actually gives $\deg(f,\mathcal O,\mathbf 1)=b\,\operatorname{sign}(\det B)$. Only nonvanishing is needed below. When there are no variables and no equations, the corresponding map is the identity of a point, with degree one.
  - Explanation: The localized degree is determined by the selected torus roots; the variable-free case has degree one.
- **stmt-thm-presentation** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-presentation)): Suppose that $\Gamma$ is residually finite and that $\mathcal D$ is a finite indexed list of words on $S$, trivial in $\Gamma$, whose chains form a free $\Lambda$-basis of $\ker\partial_1$. Thus the canonical map $$\label{eq:cycle-basis} \Lambda^{(\mathcal D)}\longrightarrow\ker\partial_1, \qquad e_z\mathrel{% \pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27FC> >> BDC}% \longmapsto \pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[z],$$ is an isomorphism. If $\Gamma$ admits a $K(\Gamma,1)$ of dimension at most two, there is an $\eta>0$ with the following property. Assign a label in $H=\operatorname{SU}(2)$ to every oriented generator edge, assign the inverse label to its reverse, and assume that every label has distance less than $\eta$ from $1$. If the product along every translate of every $z\in\mathcal D$ is $1$, then the product along every closed edge path is $1$.
  - Explanation: A hypothetical classifying space forces small labels flat on the specified cycle basis to have trivial closed-path holonomy.
- **stmt-lem-aligned-presentation** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-aligned-presentation)): Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:presentation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:presentation">4</a>, including the existence of the two-dimensional $K(\Gamma,1)$, there is a presentation $$\Gamma=\langle S,\mathcal T\mid (\rho_z)_{z\in\mathcal D},\ (r)_{r\in\mathcal R}\rangle$$ with the following properties:* 1. *every letter of $\mathcal T$ has value $1$ in $\Gamma$;* 2. *the complete relator boundary list is a $\Lambda$-basis of the cycle module of the generator graph on $S\amalg\mathcal T$;* 3. *$[\rho_z]=[z]$ as edge chains, for each $z\in\mathcal D$;* 4. *projecting the ordinary boundaries $[r]$, $r\in\mathcal R$, to the $\mathcal T$-edge coordinates gives an isomorphism $$\label{eq:ordinary-projection} \Lambda^{(\mathcal R)}\xrightarrow{\ \cong\ } \Lambda^{(\mathcal T)}.$$* *No finiteness assumption is imposed on $\mathcal T$ or $\mathcal R$.
  - Explanation: The presentation comparison aligns distinguished boundary chains while retaining an actual presentation and an invertible ordinary projection.
- **stmt-lem-exact-counts** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-exact-counts)): In the free module on all final relator indices, $$\label{eq:translated-counts} \sum_{j=1}^{n_z}\sigma_{z,j}\bar u_{z,j}e_{r_{z,j}} =e_{\rho_z}.$$
  - Explanation: The boundary-basis isomorphism makes the translated signed relator counts exact.
- **stmt-lem-quadratic-estimate** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-quadratic-estimate)): There is a constant $C\ge1$, depending only on the finite choices above, with the following property on $\Gamma$ and on every finite quotient. Suppose that all $S$-labels have distance at most $\epsilon$ from $1$, and that all translates of the ordinary relators in $\mathcal A$ have product $1$. Put $$h=\sup_{z\in\mathcal D,\ v}\|L(\rho_z,v)-1\|,$$ where the supremum is $0$ if $\mathcal D$ is empty. If $0\le h,\epsilon\le1$, then, for every $z\in\mathcal D$ and vertex $v$, $$\begin{aligned} \|L(z,v)-L(\rho_z,v)\|&\le C(h+\epsilon)h, \label{eq:quadratic-estimate}\\ \|L(z,v)-1\|&\le C\epsilon.\label{eq:short-word-estimate} \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Exact signed counts cancel the linear discrepancy, leaving a uniform quadratic error near the identity.
- **stmt-lem-finite-quotient-solvability** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-quotient-solvability)): Let $F$ be any finite quotient of $\Gamma$, and prescribe arbitrary $S$-edge labels on $F$ of distance less than $\eta$ from $1$. For every finite set $\mathcal B\subset\mathcal R$ containing $\mathcal A$, there are labels on all $\mathcal T$-edges such that $$L(r,v)=1\quad(r\in\mathcal B,\ v\in F), \qquad \max_{z\in\mathcal D,\ v\in F}\|L(\rho_z,v)-1\|<\delta.$$ The maximum over an empty distinguished list is interpreted as $0$.
  - Explanation: Each permitted finite quotient system has an extension with small distinguished products.
- **stmt-lem-geometric-seed** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-geometric-seed)): There is a finite, connected, two-dimensional flag simplicial complex $L$ with $\widetilde H_*(L;\mathbb Z)=0$ and a nontrivial homomorphism $\alpha:\pi_1(L)\longrightarrow H=\operatorname{SU}(2)$.
  - Explanation: An integrally acyclic finite flag seed retains nontrivial quaternion monodromy.
- **stmt-lem-graph-group-rf** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-graph-group-rf)): The group associated to any finite commutation graph is residually finite. In particular, $P$ and $G$ are residually finite.
  - Explanation: Finite commutation graphs give residually finite groups, and this property passes to the height kernel.
- **stmt-lem-cubical-cover** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-cubical-cover)): The space $E$ is a locally finite, contractible, three-dimensional cubical complex. Its cubes are embedded, and two intersecting cubes intersect in a common face.
  - Explanation: The cubical universal cover is contractible with embedded cubes and controlled intersections.
- **stmt-lem-acyclic-level** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-acyclic-level)): The level $X$ is a connected, integrally acyclic, two-dimensional simplicial complex. Its vertices are $G$, its edges are the integer level diagonals of squares, and its two-simplices are integer level sections of three-cubes.
  - Explanation: The height-zero level is integrally acyclic and inherits the stated simplicial cell structure.
- **stmt-prop-model** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-model)): The finite flag complex $L$, its nontrivial representation $\alpha:\pi_1(L)\to H$, and the group $G$ of <a href="#eq:height-kernel" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:height-kernel">[eq:height-kernel]</a> have the following properties.* 1. *$G$ is countable, finitely generated, and residually finite, with $\operatorname{cd}_{\mathbb Z}(G)=2$ and $\operatorname{gd}(G)\le3$.* 2. *$G$ acts freely on the integrally acyclic two-dimensional simplicial complex $X$ of <a href="#eq:zero-level" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:zero-level">[eq:zero-level]</a>. The action has finitely many cell orbits and one vertex orbit, identified with $G$ acting on itself on the left.* 3. *There is a finite generating alphabet $S$ for $G$ and a finite indexed list $\mathcal D$ of words trivial in $G$ such that, for $\Lambda=\mathbb Z[G]$, the graph of $X$ has edges $g\to gs$ and $$\label{eq:model-resolution} 0\longrightarrow\Lambda^{(\mathcal D)} \xrightarrow{\ \partial_2\ }\Lambda^{(S)} \xrightarrow{\ \partial_1\ }\Lambda \xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{aug}\ }\mathbb Z\longrightarrow0$$ is exact, with $\partial_1(e_s)=s-1$ and $\partial_2(e_z)=[z]$. Here $[z]$ is the oriented edge chain of the triangular boundary word $z$ based at $1$. In particular, the chains $[z]$, $z\in\mathcal D$, are a free $\Lambda$-basis of $\ker\partial_1$.
  - Explanation: The level action supplies a finite free resolution and the triangular boundary basis needed for the obstruction.
- **stmt-prop-small-labels** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-small-labels)): For every $\epsilon>0$, the oriented edges of $X$ admit labels $h_e\in H$ such that $$h_{\bar e}=h_e^{-1},\qquad \|h_e-1\|<\epsilon,$$ the product around every triangular two-cell is $1$, and the product around some closed edge path is different from $1$.
  - Explanation: Nonabelian transport produces arbitrarily small triangle-flat labels while retaining nontrivial closed-path holonomy.
- **stmt-lem-trace-normal-form** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-normal-form)): Every element of $P$ has a unique reduced trace, obtained by deleting inverse pairs that can be made adjacent by exchanges. Its length is the word length in the standard generators. Right multiplication by a signed standard generator changes this occurrence poset by either adjoining a maximal occurrence or deleting a maximal occurrence.
  - Explanation: Trace reduction controls word length and changes the occurrence poset by a maximal occurrence under a generator step.
- **stmt-lem-trace-direction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-trace-direction)): Suppose $g$ and $gx$ are nonidentity, where $x$ is a signed standard generator. Their direction vectors belong to a common simplex of $L$. If their word lengths are $n$ and $n+1$, in either order, then $$\label{eq:trace-direction-bound} \left\lVert b(gx)-b(g)\right\rVert_1\leq \frac{2}{n+1}.$$
  - Explanation: The occurrence assignment gives a common-simplex direction vector with reciprocal word-length variation.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that $\\Gamma$ is residually finite and that $\\mathcal D$ is a finite indexed list of words on $S$, trivial in $\\Gamma$, whose chains form a free $\\Lambda$-basis of $\\ker\\partial_1$. Thus the canonical map $$\\label{eq:cycle-basis}\n \\Lambda^{(\\mathcal D)}\\longrightarrow\\ker\\partial_1,\n \\qquad e_z\\mathrel{%\n  \\pdfliteral direct{/Span << /ActualText <FEFF27FC> >> BDC}%\n  \\longmapsto\n  \\pdfliteral direct{EMC}}[z],$$ is an isomorphism. If $\\Gamma$ admits a $K(\\Gamma,1)$ of dimension at most two, there is an $\\eta>0$ with the following property. Assign a label in $H=\\operatorname{SU}(2)$ to every oriented generator edge, assign the inverse label to its reverse, and assume that every label has distance less than $\\eta$ from $1$. If the product along every translate of every $z\\in\\mathcal D$ is $1$, then the product along every closed edge path is $1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-249.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-aligned-presentation",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:presentation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:presentation\">4</a>, including the existence of the two-dimensional $K(\\Gamma,1)$, there is a presentation $$\\Gamma=\\langle S,\\mathcal T\\mid\n        (\\rho_z)_{z\\in\\mathcal D},\\ (r)_{r\\in\\mathcal R}\\rangle$$ with the following properties:*\n\n1.  *every letter of $\\mathcal T$ has value $1$ in $\\Gamma$;*\n\n2.  *the complete relator boundary list is a $\\Lambda$-basis of the cycle module of the generator graph on $S\\amalg\\mathcal T$;*\n\n3.  *$[\\rho_z]=[z]$ as edge chains, for each $z\\in\\mathcal D$;*\n\n\n4.  *projecting the ordinary boundaries $[r]$, $r\\in\\mathcal R$, to the $\\mathcal T$-edge coordinates gives an isomorphism $$\\label{eq:ordinary-projection}\n     \\Lambda^{(\\mathcal R)}\\xrightarrow{\\ \\cong\\ }\n     \\Lambda^{(\\mathcal T)}.$$*\n\n*No finiteness assumption is imposed on $\\mathcal T$ or $\\mathcal R$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-quadratic-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C\\ge1$, depending only on the finite choices above, with the following property on $\\Gamma$ and on every finite quotient. Suppose that all $S$-labels have distance at most $\\epsilon$ from $1$, and that all translates of the ordinary relators in $\\mathcal A$ have product $1$. Put $$h=\\sup_{z\\in\\mathcal D,\\ v}|L(\\rho_z,v)-1|,$$ where the supremum is $0$ if $\\mathcal D$ is empty. If $0\\le h,\\epsilon\\le1$, then, for every $z\\in\\mathcal D$ and vertex $v$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n |L(z,v)-L(\\rho_z,v)|&\\le C(h+\\epsilon)h,\n                      \\label{eq:quadratic-estimate}\\\\\n |L(z,v)-1|&\\le C\\epsilon.\\label{eq:short-word-estimate}\n\\end{aligned}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-249.1"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-model",
        "exact_quote": "The finite flag complex $L$, its nontrivial representation $\\alpha:\\pi_1(L)\\to H$, and the group $G$ of <a href=\"#eq:height-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:height-kernel\">[eq:height-kernel]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$G$ is countable, finitely generated, and residually finite, with $\\operatorname{cd}_{\\mathbb Z}(G)=2$ and $\\operatorname{gd}(G)\\le3$.*\n\n2.  *$G$ acts freely on the integrally acyclic two-dimensional simplicial complex $X$ of <a href=\"#eq:zero-level\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:zero-level\">[eq:zero-level]</a>. The action has finitely many cell orbits and one vertex orbit, identified with $G$ acting on itself on the left.*\n\n\n3.  *There is a finite generating alphabet $S$ for $G$ and a finite indexed list $\\mathcal D$ of words trivial in $G$ such that, for $\\Lambda=\\mathbb Z[G]$, the graph of $X$ has edges $g\\to gs$ and $$\\label{eq:model-resolution}\n           0\\longrightarrow\\Lambda^{(\\mathcal D)}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\partial_2\\ }\\Lambda^{(S)}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\partial_1\\ }\\Lambda\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\mathrm{aug}\\ }\\mathbb Z\\longrightarrow0$$ is exact, with $\\partial_1(e_s)=s-1$ and $\\partial_2(e_z)=[z]$. Here $[z]$ is the oriented edge chain of the triangular boundary word $z$ based at $1$. In particular, the chains $[z]$, $z\\in\\mathcal D$, are a free $\\Lambda$-basis of $\\ker\\partial_1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-249.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-model",
          "exact_quote": "The finite flag complex $L$, its nontrivial representation $\\alpha:\\pi_1(L)\\to H$, and the group $G$ of <a href=\"#eq:height-kernel\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:height-kernel\">[eq:height-kernel]</a> have the following properties.*\n\n1.  *$G$ is countable, finitely generated, and residually finite, with $\\operatorname{cd}_{\\mathbb Z}(G)=2$ and $\\operatorname{gd}(G)\\le3$.*\n\n2.  *$G$ acts freely on the integrally acyclic two-dimensional simplicial complex $X$ of <a href=\"#eq:zero-level\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:zero-level\">[eq:zero-level]</a>. The action has finitely many cell orbits and one vertex orbit, identified with $G$ acting on itself on the left.*\n\n\n3.  *There is a finite generating alphabet $S$ for $G$ and a finite indexed list $\\mathcal D$ of words trivial in $G$ such that, for $\\Lambda=\\mathbb Z[G]$, the graph of $X$ has edges $g\\to gs$ and $$\\label{eq:model-resolution}\n           0\\longrightarrow\\Lambda^{(\\mathcal D)}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\partial_2\\ }\\Lambda^{(S)}\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\partial_1\\ }\\Lambda\n           \\xrightarrow{\\ \\mathrm{aug}\\ }\\mathbb Z\\longrightarrow0$$ is exact, with $\\partial_1(e_s)=s-1$ and $\\partial_2(e_z)=[z]$. Here $[z]$ is the oriented edge chain of the triangular boundary word $z$ based at $1$. In particular, the chains $[z]$, $z\\in\\mathcal D$, are a free $\\Lambda$-basis of $\\ker\\partial_1$.",
          "source_id": "PM-249.1"

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Integral cohomological dimension** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For a discrete group $\Gamma$, its integral cohomological dimension $\operatorname{cd}_{\mathbb Z}\Gamma$ is the projective dimension of the trivial left $\mathbb Z[\Gamma]$-module $\mathbb Z$: it measures the length of the shortest projective resolution of $\mathbb Z$.
- **Geometric dimension** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Its geometric dimension $\operatorname{gd}\Gamma$ is the least dimension of a connected CW complex with fundamental group $\Gamma$ and contractible universal cover.
- **Classifying space** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Such a complex is a *classifying space*, or a $K(\Gamma,1)$.
- **Residual finiteness** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Here *residually finite* means that every nonidentity element remains nonidentity in some finite quotient. Our group comes from a finite simplicial complex.
- **Flag complex** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A simplicial complex is *flag* if every finite set of pairwise adjacent vertices spans a simplex.
- **Right-angled Artin group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): For such a complex $L$ with vertex set $V$, the associated *right-angled Artin group* is $$A_L=\left\langle a_q\ (q\in V)\ \middle\|\ [a_q,a_r]=1\text{ if }\{q,r\}\text{ is an edge of }L\right\rangle.$$ The *height homomorphism* $\lambda:A_L\to\mathbb Z$ sends every standard generator $a_q$ to $1$.
- **Height homomorphism** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The *height homomorphism* $\lambda:A_L\to\mathbb Z$ sends every standard generator $a_q$ to $1$.
- **Integral acyclicity** ([source](#sec:introduction)): Acyclicity below always means vanishing reduced integral homology. <div class="theorem"> **Theorem 1**.
- **Path product** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *path product* multiplies the labels in traversal order; for a closed path we also call this its *holonomy*.
- **Triangle-flat labeling** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A labeling is *flat on the triangles* when every triangular boundary has product $1$.
- **Word map** ([source](#sec:degree)): A *word map* $f:H^m\to H^m$ has coordinates that are words in its $m$ arguments and their inverses, without coefficients.
- **Exponent-sum matrix** ([source](#sec:degree)): Its exponent-sum matrix $B=(B_{ij})$ records the total exponent of the $j$th argument in the $i$th word. We use ordinary mapping degree on an isolating open set.
- **Independent signed generators** ([source](#pm-h-19)): Two signed standard generators are called *independent* when their underlying vertices are distinct and adjacent in $L$.
- **Trace** ([source](#pm-h-19)): A *trace* is a word modulo exchanges of adjacent independent letters, as in the theory of partially commutative monoids (Cartier and Foata 1969, vol.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-cd | Integral cohomological dimension of the constructed group | = | $2$ | The finite acyclic flag seed specified in the theorem. |
| Q-gd | Geometric dimension of the constructed group | = | $3$ | Ordinary classifying spaces, regardless of cell cardinality. |
| Q-det | Determinant of the seed exponent-sum matrix | = | $1$ | The displayed exponent-sum rows. |
| Q-sin | Sine of the seed angle | > | $1/2$ | The source fixes $\theta=\pi/5$. |
| Q-real | Real part of the seed quaternion product | = | $1/2$ | Imaginary unit quaternions with the prescribed dot product. |
| Q-threshold-quadratic | Scaled quadratic threshold | < | $\tfrac12$ | Positive thresholds chosen after the uniform constant. |
| Q-threshold-linear | Scaled linear threshold | < | $\tfrac{\delta}{2}$ | Positive thresholds chosen after the uniform constant. |
| Q-direction | Direction-vector variation | ≤ | $\frac{2}{n+1}$ | Nonidentity adjacent words with lengths differing by a generator step. |
| Q-triangle | Diameter of a mapped triangular boundary | ≤ | $8/N$ | The translated graph map and every triangular boundary. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R-degree** [claim](#stmt-lem-localized-degree) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-localized-degree): Equivariant perturbations and signed degree counts. -> Nonzero localized degree.
- **R-align** [claim](#stmt-lem-aligned-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-aligned-presentation): Finite-support module shears realized by relator words. -> An actual presentation with aligned distinguished boundary chains.
- **R-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-exact-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-exact-counts): Injectivity of the complete relator boundary map. -> Exact translated signed counts.
- **R-estimate** [claim](#stmt-lem-quadratic-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-quadratic-estimate): Quaternion commutator estimates and exact signed cancellation. -> A uniform quadratic discrepancy estimate.
- **R-finite** [claim](#stmt-lem-finite-quotient-solvability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-quotient-solvability): Independent exponent vectors, localization and degree-preserving deformation. -> An extension with small distinguished products.
- **R-compact** [claim](#stmt-thm-presentation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finite-quotient-solvability-2): Residual-finite separation and finite-coordinate product compactness. -> Trivial closed-path holonomy under the hypothetical classifying-space assumption.
- **R-seed** [claim](#stmt-lem-geometric-seed) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-geometric-seed): An integral cellular boundary calculation, quaternion representation and flag triangulation. -> A finite acyclic flag seed with nontrivial monodromy.
- **R-cover** [claim](#stmt-lem-cubical-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-cubical-cover): Induction over commutation graphs and cubical face analysis. -> A contractible cubical universal cover.
- **R-level** [claim](#stmt-lem-acyclic-level) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-acyclic-level): Ascending and descending links followed by Mayer–Vietoris. -> An integrally acyclic level complex.
- **R-model** [claim](#stmt-prop-model) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-model): Free cellular chains and subgroup dimension comparison. -> The height kernel and its triangular boundary basis.
- **R-direction** [claim](#stmt-lem-trace-direction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-direction): Reduced traces and unchanged principal downsets. -> Common-simplex direction vectors with reciprocal variation.
- **R-labels** [claim](#stmt-prop-small-labels) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-trace-direction-2): Slowly varying translated maps and pullback of equivariant transport. -> Arbitrarily small triangle-flat labels with nontrivial closed-path holonomy.
- **R-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Contradict the presentation obstruction using nontrivial small transport. -> Separation of integral cohomological and geometric dimensions.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **seed-boundary**: The exponent-sum boundary of the seed presentation is an integral isomorphism.
- **ordinary-dimensions**: The constructed height kernel has integral cohomological dimension two and geometric dimension three.
- **small-holonomy**: Triangle-flat labels can have nontrivial closed-path product despite integral acyclicity.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **acyclic-contractible**: Treat an acyclic complex as a classifying space. -> Acyclicity alone leaves the universal-cover requirement unproved.
- **abelian-labels**: Replace the quaternion target by an abelian target while retaining the holonomy obstruction. -> Closed-path products become trivial.
- **finite-global**: Use the finite seed boundary check to certify the complete dimension obstruction. -> The check does not establish the presentation comparison or global transport.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The seed presentation supplies a finite constructive integral boundary calculation. This check establishes its determinant and inverse, while the classifying-space obstruction and small-label transport remain outside the executed calculation.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of pairs of relator words using x, X, y, Y.
# PRECONDITIONS: At most 512 pairs; each word has length at most 64.
# OUTPUTS: Exponent-sum rows, exact determinant and integral inverse or null.
# TIME: Linear in total input word length.
# SPACE: Linear in the number of pairs.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact FLINT integers; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise ValueError; failed inverse witness raises AssertionError.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpz, fmpz_mat
pairs = json.load(sys.stdin)
if not isinstance(pairs, list) or len(pairs) > 512:
    raise ValueError("invalid batch")
answers = []
for pair in pairs:
    if not isinstance(pair, list) or len(pair) != 2:
        raise ValueError("invalid pair")
    rows = []
    for word in pair:
        if not isinstance(word, str) or len(word) > 64 or set(word) - set("xXyY"):
            raise ValueError("invalid word")
        ex = fmpz(0)
        ey = fmpz(0)
        for ch in word:
            sign = 1 if ch.islower() else -1
            if ch.lower() == "x":
                ex += sign
            else:
                ey += sign
        rows.append([int(ex), int(ey)])
    a, b = rows[0]
    c, d = rows[1]
    det = int(fmpz_mat(rows).det())
    inverse = None
    if abs(det) == 1:
        inverse = [[d // det, -b // det], [-c // det, a // det]]
        assert fmpz_mat(rows) * fmpz_mat(inverse) == fmpz_mat([[1,0],[0,1]])
    answers.append({"rows": rows, "det": det, "inverse": inverse})
print(json.dumps(answers, sort_keys=True))
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | A mathematical group-and-complex representation can retain cohomological and geometric dimension as distinct properties and use the constructed height kernel as a regression example. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The constructed classifying-space obstruction does not supply a simulation model, update law or game mechanic. |
| tools | True | weak | inspirational | inferred | Mathematical inspection tools can expose the difference between an acyclic cellular model and a contractible universal cover when presenting dimension claims. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Deletion of exchange-adjacent inverse occurrences gives a finite reduction procedure for words in the stated graph group; uniqueness supplies a correctness condition. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite occurrence poset stores the dependency information of a trace and supports the stated maximal-occurrence update under generator multiplication. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite-word construction and compactness proof give no accelerator architecture, parallel execution contract or resource bound. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Residual finiteness and nontrivial holonomy are mathematical separation properties here; no security assumption or cryptographic protocol is supplied. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The direction-vector and transport arguments establish geometric properties without a learning objective, statistical model or training guarantee. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proof establishes existence through degree and compactness without an objective function, optimisation procedure or approximation guarantee. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite-coordinate compactness produces a mathematical extension over arbitrary index sets; it does not provide a distributed protocol or consistency guarantee. |
| other (Algebraic topology and geometric group theory) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The constructed height kernel separates integral cohomological dimension from geometric dimension, while the acyclic level model provides a free resolution without being a classifying-space certificate. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **ordinary-integral-scope** (formal_scope): The dimension statements concern ordinary integral cohomological dimension and ordinary geometric dimension. Blocked use: ['Applying the conclusions to proper-action dimensions or dimensions relative to a family of subgroups.', 'Transferring the integral cohomological conclusion to another coefficient system without proof.']
- **constructed-group-scope** (counterexample_scope): The dimension separation is established for the constructed height kernel associated to the specified finite acyclic flag triangulation. Blocked use: ['Assigning the same dimensions to an arbitrary finitely generated residually finite group.']
- **acyclic-is-not-contractible** (edge_case): A free action on an acyclic complex gives homological information but does not by itself certify a classifying space. Blocked use: ['Treating vanishing reduced integral homology as a certificate of contractibility.', 'Ruling out every classifying space merely by showing that a particular acyclic model is noncontractible.']
- **comparison-basis-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The comparison requires residual finiteness and the specified finite indexed list of trivial words to give a free basis of the cycle module, including independence. Blocked use: ['Using a spanning list with unproved independence in place of the required basis.', 'Applying the comparison without residual finiteness or without the hypothetical classifying-space hypothesis.']
- **chains-are-not-products** (edge_case): Equal boundary chains do not determine equal path products in the nonabelian target. Blocked use: ['Replacing the actual presentation and word identities by equality of boundary chains alone.']
- **triangles-not-a-presentation** (hypothesis): The triangular words being trivial and supplying a boundary basis does not establish that they present the group. Blocked use: ['Treating the triangular words alone as an actual presentation when evaluating every closed path.']
- **localized-degree-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The localized degree result requires a nonsingular exponent-sum matrix and an invariant isolating open set containing the identity. Blocked use: ['Inferring a solution in a chosen region solely from the global degree.', 'Applying localization to a set whose boundary meets the identity fiber or whose conjugation invariance is unproved.']
- **uniform-smallness** (hypothesis): The quadratic estimate applies with the stated smallness regime and with every required ordinary relator equation holding exactly. Blocked use: ['Replacing exact ordinary relator equations by small numerical residuals.', 'Using arbitrary thresholds without establishing the required inequalities.']
- **compactness-not-finite-search** (nonconstructive): The global extension uses compactness over an unrestricted index set; the presentation being compared need not have countably many generators. Blocked use: ['Claiming that a finite enumeration certifies the global extension or its arbitrary-cardinality scope.', 'Reading the existence argument as an implemented terminating solver for all labels.']
- **no-single-quotient-descent** (edge_case): Finite quotients preserve the labels needed for each finite constraint collection; a single quotient need not carry the original entire labeling. Blocked use: ['Assuming that the original labeling descends to one finite quotient.', 'Using a quotient that identifies vertices bearing inconsistent prescribed labels.']
- **nonabelian-target** (hypothesis): The nontrivial holonomy construction requires a nonabelian target and the nontrivial representation of the seed fundamental group. Blocked use: ['Substituting an abelian target while retaining the claimed nontrivial closed-path product.']
- **finite-support-arbitrary-index** (formal_scope): Arbitrary generator and relator index sets are allowed, while individual words and chains retain finite support. Blocked use: ['Replacing direct sums by products or admitting infinite relator words without a separate argument.']
- **finite-seed-certificate-scope** (formal_scope): The exponent-sum determinant verifies the cellular boundary of the seed presentation complex; that arithmetic alone does not prove the global geometric-dimension obstruction. Blocked use: ['Promoting a seed boundary calculation to a certificate of the full dimension theorem.']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 249 (`lean/docs/249.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [A finitely generated counterexample to the Eilenberg–Ganea conjecture](../../preprints/A-finitely-generated-counterexample-to-the-Eilenberg-Ganea-conjecture-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The Eilenberg–Ganea conjecture predicts that a group of integral cohomological dimension two has a two-dimensional classifying space. The formalization constructs a finitely generated residually finite group of cohomological dimension two that has a three-dimensional classifying space but no two-dimensional one. Thus its geometric dimension is three, contradicting the conjecture.

Comparator source `EilenbergGanea.lean` (4900 bytes, sha256 `2cac9f3dccc0517a9a162bee18f3b2a470d728cad57714f7492f1ee95233bf1a`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open Classical Set Topology

namespace EilenbergGanea

/-- The signed words encode the relators `x²y⁻⁵` and `x²(yx⁻¹)³`. -/
def seedWord : Bool → List (Bool × Bool)
  | false => [(false, true), (false, true), (true, false), (true, false),
      (true, false), (true, false), (true, false)]
  | true => [(false, true), (false, true), (true, true), (false, false),
      (true, true), (false, false), (true, true), (false, false)]

def boundaryLength (relation : Bool) : ℕ := 3 * (seedWord relation).length

abbrev RoseVertex := Option (Bool × Fin 2)
abbrev SeedVertex := RoseVertex ⊕ Bool
abbrev CircleEdge := Bool × Fin 3
abbrev Sector := (relation : Bool) × Fin (boundaryLength relation)
abbrev SeedEdge := CircleEdge ⊕ Sector
abbrev SeedCell := SeedVertex ⊕ (SeedEdge ⊕ Sector)

def circleStart (edge : CircleEdge) : RoseVertex :=
  ![none, some (edge.1, 0), some (edge.1, 1)] edge.2

def circleEnd (edge : CircleEdge) : RoseVertex :=
  ![some (edge.1, 0), some (edge.1, 1), none] edge.2

def sectorLetter (sector : Sector) : Bool × Bool :=
  (seedWord sector.1).get ⟨sector.2.val / 3, by
    have bound := sector.2.isLt
    dsimp [boundaryLength] at bound
    omega⟩

def sectorCircleEdge (sector : Sector) : CircleEdge :=
  ((sectorLetter sector).1, if (sectorLetter sector).2 then
    ⟨sector.2.val % 3, Nat.mod_lt _ (by decide)⟩ else
    ⟨2 - sector.2.val % 3, by omega⟩)

def sectorStart (sector : Sector) : RoseVertex :=
  if (sectorLetter sector).2 then circleStart (sectorCircleEdge sector)
  else circleEnd (sectorCircleEdge sector)

def nextSector (sector : Sector) : Sector :=
  ⟨sector.1, ⟨(sector.2.val + 1) % boundaryLength sector.1,
    Nat.mod_lt _ (by have := sector.2.isLt; omega)⟩⟩

def edgeVertices : SeedEdge → Finset SeedVertex
  | .inl edge => {.inl (circleStart edge), .inl (circleEnd edge)}
  | .inr sector => {.inr sector.1, .inl (sectorStart sector)}

def sectorEdges (sector : Sector) : Finset SeedEdge :=
  {.inl (sectorCircleEdge sector), .inr sector, .inr (nextSector sector)}

def sectorVertices (sector : Sector) : Finset SeedVertex :=
  (sectorEdges sector).biUnion edgeVertices

def properFace : SeedCell → SeedCell → Prop
  | .inl vertex, .inr (.inl edge) => vertex ∈ edgeVertices edge
  | .inl vertex, .inr (.inr sector) => vertex ∈ sectorVertices sector
  | .inr (.inl edge), .inr (.inr sector) => edge ∈ sectorEdges sector
  | _, _ => False

/-- Comparability graph of the seven vertices, 51 edges and 45 sectors. -/
def seedGraph : SimpleGraph SeedCell where
  Adj first second := properFace first second ∨ properFace second first
  symm := ⟨fun _ _ adjacent => adjacent.elim Or.inr Or.inl⟩
  loopless := ⟨by
    intro cell
    rcases cell with vertex | cell
    · simp [properFace]
    · rcases cell with edge | sector <;> simp [properFace]⟩

def artinRelations {Vertex : Type*} (graph : SimpleGraph Vertex) : Set (FreeGroup Vertex) :=
  {word | ∃ first second, graph.Adj first second ∧
    word = FreeGroup.of first * FreeGroup.of second *
      (FreeGroup.of first)⁻¹ * (FreeGroup.of second)⁻¹}

abbrev ArtinGroup {Vertex : Type*} (graph : SimpleGraph Vertex) :=
  PresentedGroup (artinRelations graph)

def height {Vertex : Type*} (graph : SimpleGraph Vertex) :
    ArtinGroup graph →* Multiplicative ℤ :=
  PresentedGroup.toGroup (f := fun _ : Vertex => Multiplicative.ofAdd (1 : ℤ)) (by
    rintro word ⟨first, second, _, rfl⟩
    simp)

abbrev SourceGroup := (height seedGraph).ker

/-- Projective dimension of the trivial integral left group-ring module. -/
def cohomologicalDimension (GroupType : Type*) [Group GroupType] : WithBot ℕ∞ :=
  CategoryTheory.projectiveDimension
    (ModuleCat.of (MonoidAlgebra ℤ GroupType)
      (Representation.trivial ℤ GroupType ℤ).asModule)

universe u

/-- Ordinary classifying CW spaces, with no bound on the cardinality of their cells. -/
def HasClassifyingSpace (GroupType : Type*) [Group GroupType] (dimension : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ (Space : Type u) (_ : TopologicalSpace Space) (_ : T2Space Space)
    (_ : Topology.CWComplex (Set.univ : Set Space)),
    (∀ degree : ℕ, dimension < degree →
      IsEmpty (Topology.CWComplex.cell (Set.univ : Set Space) degree)) ∧
    PathConnectedSpace Space ∧
    ∃ basepoint : Space, Nonempty (FundamentalGroup Space basepoint ≃* GroupType) ∧
    ∃ (Cover : Type u) (_ : TopologicalSpace Cover), ContractibleSpace Cover ∧
    ∃ projection : Cover → Space, IsCoveringMap projection ∧ Function.Surjective projection

theorem source_main :
    Group.FG SourceGroup ∧ Group.ResiduallyFinite SourceGroup ∧
    cohomologicalDimension SourceGroup = 2 ∧
    HasClassifyingSpace.{0} SourceGroup 3 ∧
    ¬ HasClassifyingSpace.{u} SourceGroup 2 := by
  sorry

end EilenbergGanea

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We construct a finitely generated residually finite group of integral cohomological dimension two and geometric dimension three, disproving the Eilenberg–Ganea conjecture.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
